Yankovic released the song as a free download on his MySpace profile, as well as his official website, and plays it in concert, since it was not Blunt himself objecting to the parody.[149] Yankovic referenced the incident in his video for "White & Nerdy" when he depicts himself vandalizing Atlantic Records' Wikipedia article. 

Yankovic was considering a complete polka medley with only [U2](/wiki/U2 "U2") songs, but the band's publisher did not accept the terms.[101] Similarly, he had included [Weezer](/wiki/Weezer "Weezer")'s "[Buddy Holly](/wiki/Buddy_Holly_\(song\) "Buddy Holly \(song\)")" in a polka medley, but had to pull it when the band's publisher refused to receive partial rates.[101]

Yankovic had approached [Beck](/wiki/Beck "Beck") asking for permission to parody his song "[Loser](/wiki/Loser_\(Beck_song\) "Loser \(Beck song\)")", which Yankovic had created called "Schmoozer". At the time, Beck was just entering the music industry, and did not want his reputation to be seen as a one-hit wonder and refused the parody. Beck stated in 2022 that he wished he had given Yankovic permission, saying "I think it would have been an amazing video, I'm actually really sad it didn't happen."[150] Although a parody never happened, "Loser" was later used in a polka medley. 

Yankovic had planned to make a parody based on "[Hedwig's Theme](/wiki/Hedwig%27s_Theme "Hedwig's Theme")" from the _[Harry Potter](/wiki/Harry_Potter_\(film_series\) "Harry Potter \(film series\)")_ film series, but had been refused by [Warner Bros. Pictures](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Pictures "Warner Bros. Pictures"). Yankovic said that perhaps if he did a parody based on a franchise, "it's usually better just to do it and ask for forgiveness rather than permission".[151]

### Live performances

[](/wiki/File:Atlantic_record_sucks_shirt_your_pitiful_aug_8th_2007_ohio_state_fair.JPG)Weird Al wearing his "Atlantic Records Sucks" shirt during a performance of "[You're Pitiful](/wiki/You%27re_Pitiful "You're Pitiful")", in 2007, at the [Ohio State Fair](/wiki/Ohio_State_Fair "Ohio State Fair")

Yankovic often describes his live concert performances as "a rock and comedy multimedia extravaganza"[152] with an audience that "ranges from toddlers to geriatrics".[99] Apart from Yankovic and his band performing his classic and contemporary hits, staples of Yankovic's live performances include a medley of parodies, many costume changes between songs, and a video screen on which various clips are played during the costume changes.[152] A concert from Yankovic's 1999 tour, "Touring with Scissors", for the _[Running with Scissors](/wiki/Running_with_Scissors_\(%22Weird_Al%22_Yankovic_album\) "Running with Scissors \("Weird Al" Yankovic album\)")_ album was released on VHS in 1999 and on DVD in 2000.[7] Titled _"Weird Al" Yankovic Live!_ , the concert was recorded at the [Marin County Civic Center](/wiki/Marin_County_Civic_Center "Marin County Civic Center") in [San Rafael, California](/wiki/San_Rafael,_California "San Rafael, California"), on October 2, 1999.[153] For legal reasons, video clips (apart from those for Yankovic's own music videos) could not be shown for the home release, and unreleased parodies were removed from the parody medley for the performance.[154]

In 2003, Yankovic toured overseas for the first time. Before 2003, Yankovic and his band had toured only the United States and parts of Canada.[9] Following the success of _[Poodle Hat](/wiki/Poodle_Hat "Poodle Hat")_ in Australia, Yankovic performed eleven shows in Australia's major capital cities and regional areas in October of that year.[155] Yankovic returned to Australia and toured New Zealand for the first time in 2007 to support the _[Straight Outta Lynwood](/wiki/Straight_Outta_Lynwood "Straight Outta Lynwood")_ album. On September 8, 2007, Yankovic performed his 1,000th live show at [Idaho Falls, Idaho](/wiki/Idaho_Falls,_Idaho "Idaho Falls, Idaho").[9]

Yankovic has invited members of the [501st Legion](/wiki/501st_Legion "501st Legion") on stage during performances of his _Star Wars_ -themed songs "[Yoda](/wiki/Yoda_\(song\) "Yoda \(song\)")" and "The Saga Begins", recruiting members of local garrisons (club chapters) while on tour. In appreciation, the 501st inducted Yankovic as a "Friend of the Legion" in September 2007.[156]

He performed his first ever European mini-tour, including an appearance at the [All Tomorrow's Parties](/wiki/All_Tomorrow%27s_Parties_\(music_festival\) "All Tomorrow's Parties \(music festival\)") music festival in [Minehead](/wiki/Minehead "Minehead"), England in December 2010. Yankovic was picked to perform by the Canadian band [Godspeed You! Black Emperor](/wiki/Godspeed_You!_Black_Emperor "Godspeed You! Black Emperor"), who curated the festival's lineup. Yankovic played three other dates in the UK around his festival appearance before performing a single date in the Netherlands.[157]

A second concert film, _["Weird Al" Yankovic Live!: The Alpocalypse Tour](/wiki/%22Weird_Al%22_Yankovic_Live!:_The_Alpocalypse_Tour ""Weird Al" Yankovic Live!: The Alpocalypse Tour")_ , aired on [Comedy Central](/wiki/Comedy_Central "Comedy Central") on October 1, 2011, and was released on Blu-ray and DVD three days later. The concert was filmed at [Massey Hall](/wiki/Massey_Hall "Massey Hall") in Toronto, Canada, during Yankovic's tour supporting the album _Alpocalypse_. As before, video clips (apart from those for his own videos) and unreleased songs were edited out for legal reasons. 

Yankovic performed [George Harrison](/wiki/George_Harrison "George Harrison")'s "[What Is Life](/wiki/What_Is_Life "What Is Life")" at the live-recorded [George Fest](/wiki/George_Fest "George Fest") (Los Angeles, 2014). DVD and Blu-Ray CD combos of the concert honoring Harrison became available in early 2016. 

Following the release of _Mandatory Fun_ , Yankovic toured across the United States, Canada, and selected overseas venues in the "[Mandatory World Tour](/wiki/Mandatory_World_Tour "Mandatory World Tour")" from 2015 through 2016, principally featuring songs from this album. After taking a year off, Yankovic returned to tour in the United States and Canada from February to June 2018 in "[The Ridiculously Self-Indulgent, Ill-Advised Vanity Tour](/wiki/The_Ridiculously_Self-Indulgent,_Ill-Advised_Vanity_Tour "The Ridiculously Self-Indulgent, Ill-Advised Vanity Tour")". On this tour, he performed mostly original songs (not parodies) and did not use costumes, props, or video screens. Comedian [Emo Philips](/wiki/Emo_Philips "Emo Philips") was the opening act.[158][159] A further staple of this tour was Yankovic's cover performance of a different famous song at each venue, which Yankovic stated was something he and his band enjoyed doing.[160]

Starting in June 2019, Yankovic went on his "Strings Attached Tour", where he performed every show backed by a forty-one piece orchestra assembled from local musicians.[161] The tour was inspired by a 2016 performance he did with the [Hollywood Bowl Orchestra](/wiki/Hollywood_Bowl_Orchestra "Hollywood Bowl Orchestra"), which he considered a "religious experience" and sought to replicate on tour.[162] The shows were generally much shorter, as under union rules Yankovic could only perform 90 minutes per show with an orchestra, requiring him to select songs that he felt would be ones that he had either long wanted to perform with an orchestra, such as the deep-cut "Harvey the Wonder Hamster" from _Alapalooza_ , or that fit best with the orchestra backing.[162][163] Yankovic had the shows open with the orchestra performing a few instrumental themes, seemingly giving the concert a high-brow quality, before he and his band entered and played his songs backed by the orchestra. The concerts finished with a large flashy production of his _[Star Wars](/wiki/Star_Wars "Star Wars")_ songs, including "The Saga Begins" and "[Yoda](/wiki/Yoda_\(song\) "Yoda \(song\)")".[164][165]

Yankovic toured again in 2022, following up from his 2018 tour with the "Unfortunate Return Of The Ridiculously Self-Indulgent, Ill-Advised Vanity Tour", focusing on his lesser-known songs. The tour included 133 shows, concluding with Yankovic's first performance at [Carnegie Hall](/wiki/Carnegie_Hall "Carnegie Hall") in October 2022. Yankovic said "I've loved doing every single incarnation of my live show, but honestly the Vanity tour is the most fun I've ever had on stage, so I've been dying to get back out there and torture everybody with it once again!"[166]

Yankovic toured in 2025 with the "Bigger and Weirder Tour", with 65 different shows, including his first ever performance at [Madison Square Garden](/wiki/Madison_Square_Garden "Madison Square Garden").[167]

### Legacy and influence

With "[Word Crimes](/wiki/Word_Crimes "Word Crimes")" from _Mandatory Fun_ debuting at No. 39 on the [Billboard Hot 100](/wiki/Billboard_Hot_100 "Billboard Hot 100") in 2014, Yankovic became the third musical artist after Michael Jackson and Madonna to have a song in the Top 40 of the Billboard Hot 100 over each decade since the 1980s, his other Top 40 songs being "Eat It", "Smells Like Nirvana", and "White & Nerdy".[168] Since then, only [U2](/wiki/U2 "U2") and [Kenny G](/wiki/Kenny_G "Kenny G") have also entered this group.[169] _Billboard_ named Yankovic No. 15 of the top 100 music video artists of all time in an August 2020 compilation, addressing that alongside his musical fame, "his accompanying video parodies are a vital part of the recipe".[170]

With his four-decade career, Yankovic's work has also influenced newer artists. [Andy Samberg](/wiki/Andy_Samberg "Andy Samberg") of the group [The Lonely Island](/wiki/The_Lonely_Island "The Lonely Island") considered Yankovic an influence during his childhood.[19] [Lin-Manuel Miranda](/wiki/Lin-Manuel_Miranda "Lin-Manuel Miranda") directly credits Yankovic as an influence on his musical _[Hamilton](/wiki/Hamilton_\(musical\) "Hamilton \(musical\)")_.[19] Television producer [Michael Schur](/wiki/Michael_Schur "Michael Schur") considered that Yankovic's music represented a "deep egalitarian spirit of our culture" that allowed his comedy writers to reflect on society within his shows.[19]

Certain polka musicians (such as Nick Smyth of [the Dreadnoughts](/wiki/The_Dreadnoughts "The Dreadnoughts")) have criticized Yankovic's use of polka as comedy, saying that it contributes to the "dumb Euro" stereotype of portraying Central and Eastern European culture as goofy or unintelligent. Smyth says that Yankovic has "unwittingly taught the next two generations that polka is just a mashup comedy style", detracting from and reversing the community-building role that social dance music such as polka has always fulfilled.[171]

In 2020, Mark Riedl, a researcher at [Georgia Tech](/wiki/Georgia_Tech "Georgia Tech"), created an algorithm that generates lyrics to match the [rhyme](/wiki/Rhyme_scheme "Rhyme scheme") and syllable schemes of preexisting songs. The algorithm was called "Weird [A.I.](/wiki/Artificial_intelligence "Artificial intelligence") Yankovic" in reference to Yankovic's similar song parodies.[172]

## Other works

### Films

#### _UHF_

Main article: [UHF (film)](/wiki/UHF_\(film\) "UHF \(film\)")

In 1989, Yankovic starred in a feature film called _UHF_ , co-written by himself and manager Jay Levey and filmed in [Tulsa, Oklahoma](/wiki/Tulsa,_Oklahoma "Tulsa, Oklahoma"). A satire of the television and film industries, also starring [Michael Richards](/wiki/Michael_Richards "Michael Richards"), [Fran Drescher](/wiki/Fran_Drescher "Fran Drescher"), and [Victoria Jackson](/wiki/Victoria_Jackson "Victoria Jackson"), it brought floundering studio [Orion](/wiki/Orion_Pictures "Orion Pictures") their highest [test scores](/wiki/Test_screening "Test screening") since the film _[RoboCop](/wiki/RoboCop "RoboCop")_.[173] However, it was unsuccessful in theaters due to both poor critical reception and competition from other summer blockbusters at the time such as _[Indiana Jones and the Last Crusade](/wiki/Indiana_Jones_and_the_Last_Crusade "Indiana Jones and the Last Crusade")_ , _[Lethal Weapon 2](/wiki/Lethal_Weapon_2 "Lethal Weapon 2")_ , _[Batman](/wiki/Batman_\(1989_film\) "Batman \(1989 film\)")_ and _[Licence to Kill](/wiki/Licence_to_Kill "Licence to Kill")_.[174] The failure of the film left Yankovic in a three-year slump, which was later broken by his inspiration to compose "Smells Like Nirvana".[42]

The film has since become a [cult classic](/wiki/Cult_film "Cult film");[175] fans bought it on eBay for high prices before it came out on DVD.[176] Yankovic occasionally shows clips from the film at his concerts (to which [MGM](/wiki/Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer "Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer"), the film's current owner, initially objected in the form of a [cease and desist](/wiki/Cease_and_desist "Cease and desist") letter).[177] In an apparent attempt to make it more accessible to overseas audiences, where the term [UHF](/wiki/Ultra_high_frequency "Ultra high frequency") is used less frequently to describe TV broadcasts, the film was titled _The Vidiot From UHF_ in Australia and parts of Europe. 

_UHF_ shows the creation of Yankovic's signature food—the Twinkie Wiener Sandwich. The snack consists of an overturned [Twinkie](/wiki/Twinkie "Twinkie") split open as a makeshift bun, a [hot dog](/wiki/Hot_dog "Hot dog"), and [Easy Cheese](/wiki/Easy_Cheese "Easy Cheese") put together and dipped in milk before eating. Yankovic has stated that he has switched to using [tofu](/wiki/Tofu "Tofu") hot dogs since becoming a vegetarian, but still enjoys the occasional Twinkie Wiener Sandwich.[178]

#### _Weird: The Al Yankovic Story_

Main article: [Weird: The Al Yankovic Story](/wiki/Weird:_The_Al_Yankovic_Story "Weird: The Al Yankovic Story")

In 2010, [Eric Appel](/wiki/Eric_Appel "Eric Appel") produced a _[Funny or Die](/wiki/Funny_or_Die "Funny or Die")_ trailer for _Weird: The Al Yankovic Story_ , a fictional biographical film that parodies other films based on musicians; Yankovic (played by [Aaron Paul](/wiki/Aaron_Paul "Aaron Paul")) is seen hiding his "weirdness" from his parents ([Gary Cole](/wiki/Gary_Cole "Gary Cole") and [Mary Steenburgen](/wiki/Mary_Steenburgen "Mary Steenburgen")), making it big using song parodies with the help of Dr. Demento ([Patton Oswalt](/wiki/Patton_Oswalt "Patton Oswalt")), falling in and out of love with Madonna ([Olivia Wilde](/wiki/Olivia_Wilde "Olivia Wilde")), and fading into alcoholism and being arrested, at which point his father finally admits he is "weird" as well. Yankovic himself plays a music producer in the short.[50][179][180][181] Yankovic and Appel announced in January 2022 that they would be making a full-length [biopic of the same name](/wiki/Weird:_The_Al_Yankovic_Story "Weird: The Al Yankovic Story") based on the trailer, starring [Daniel Radcliffe](/wiki/Daniel_Radcliffe "Daniel Radcliffe") as Yankovic, [Evan Rachel Wood](/wiki/Evan_Rachel_Wood "Evan Rachel Wood") as Madonna, and [Rainn Wilson](/wiki/Rainn_Wilson "Rainn Wilson") as Dr. Demento.[182] The film premiered at the [Toronto International Film Festival](/wiki/Toronto_International_Film_Festival "Toronto International Film Festival") in September 2022, and was released on the Roku Channel in November 2022. [The film's soundtrack album](/wiki/Weird:_The_Al_Yankovic_Story_\(soundtrack\) "Weird: The Al Yankovic Story \(soundtrack\)") includes several re-recorded parodies featured in the film along with an original song "Now You Know".[183] Among other nominations and awards, the film won the [Best Television Movie](/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Award_for_Outstanding_Television_Movie "Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Television Movie") at the [75th Primetime Creative Arts Emmy Awards](/wiki/75th_Primetime_Creative_Arts_Emmy_Awards "75th Primetime Creative Arts Emmy Awards").[184]

### Live television

In 2012, Yankovic extensively featured in the [sixth-season](/wiki/30_Rock_\(season_6\) "30 Rock \(season 6\)") episode of _[30 Rock](/wiki/30_Rock "30 Rock")_ , "[Kidnapped by Danger](/wiki/Kidnapped_by_Danger "Kidnapped by Danger")", in which [Jenna Maroney](/wiki/Jenna_Maroney "Jenna Maroney") desperately tries to come up with a "'Weird Al'-proof" song.[185] In 2014, he performed at the [66th Primetime Emmy Awards](/wiki/66th_Primetime_Emmy_Awards "66th Primetime Emmy Awards"), singing a comedic medley of songs based on the themes of several Emmy-nominated shows, including _[Mad Men](/wiki/Mad_Men "Mad Men")_ and _[Game of Thrones](/wiki/Game_of_Thrones "Game of Thrones")_.[186] Yankovic and Miranda appeared on the July 21, 2025, episode of _[The Late Show with Stephen Colbert](/wiki/The_Late_Show_with_Stephen_Colbert "The Late Show with Stephen Colbert")_ a few days after news that the program would be cancelled, showing support for Colbert along with a number of guest cameos from other late night talk show hosts.[187]

### Animation and voice work

See also: [_Star Wars Detours_](/wiki/Star_Wars_Detours "Star Wars Detours")

Yankovic has done voice-overs for several animated series. He appeared in a 2003 episode of _[The Simpsons](/wiki/The_Simpsons "The Simpsons")_ , singing "The Ballad of Homer & Marge" (a parody of [John Mellencamp](/wiki/John_Mellencamp "John Mellencamp")'s "[Jack & Diane](/wiki/Jack_%26_Diane "Jack & Diane")") with his band. The episode, "[Three Gays of the Condo](/wiki/Three_Gays_of_the_Condo "Three Gays of the Condo")", in which [Marge](/wiki/Marge_Simpson "Marge Simpson") hires Yankovic to sing the aforementioned song to [Homer](/wiki/Homer_Simpson "Homer Simpson") in an attempt to reconcile their marriage, later won an [Emmy Award](/wiki/Emmy_Award "Emmy Award") for "Outstanding Animated Program (For Programming Less Than One Hour)". Yankovic also had cameos in a 2008 episode, titled "[That '90s Show](/wiki/That_%2790s_Show_\(The_Simpsons\) "That '90s Show \(The Simpsons\)")", during which he records a parody of Homer's grunge hit "Shave Me" titled "Brain Freeze" (Homer's song, "Shave Me", was itself a parody of Nirvana's "[Rape Me](/wiki/Rape_Me "Rape Me")"), and in the [couch gag](/wiki/Couch_gag "Couch gag") of a 2023 episode, titled "[Hostile Kirk Place](/wiki/Hostile_Kirk_Place "Hostile Kirk Place")", making Yankovic one of only a handful of celebrities to appear three times on the show playing themselves. 

He appeared in the animated [Adult Swim](/wiki/Adult_Swim "Adult Swim") show _Robot Chicken_ , which provided him with a music video for the song "Weasel Stomping Day".[188][189] Yankovic is the voice for [Squid Hat](/wiki/List_of_characters_in_The_Grim_Adventures_of_Billy_and_Mandy#Toadblatt's_Summer_School_of_Sorcery "List of characters in The Grim Adventures of Billy and Mandy") on the [Cartoon Network](/wiki/Cartoon_Network "Cartoon Network") show _[The Grim Adventures of Billy& Mandy](/wiki/The_Grim_Adventures_of_Billy_%26_Mandy "The Grim Adventures of Billy & Mandy")_. He is also the announcer of the cartoon's eponymous [video game adaptation](/wiki/The_Grim_Adventures_of_Billy_%26_Mandy_\(video_game\) "The Grim Adventures of Billy & Mandy \(video game\)"). 

Yankovic had a guest appearance voicing Wreck-Gar, a [waste collection vehicle](/wiki/Waste_collection_vehicle "Waste collection vehicle") Transformer in the _[Transformers: Animated](/wiki/Transformers:_Animated "Transformers: Animated")_ cartoon series;[190] previously, Yankovic's "Dare to Be Stupid" song was featured in the 1986 animated film _[The Transformers: The Movie](/wiki/The_Transformers:_The_Movie "The Transformers: The Movie")_ , during the sequence in which Wreck-Gar was first introduced; as such, the song is referenced in the episode. He also plays local TV talent show host Uncle Muscles on several episodes of _[Tim and Eric Awesome Show, Great Job!](/wiki/Tim_and_Eric_Awesome_Show,_Great_Job! "Tim and Eric Awesome Show, Great Job!")_ along with other appearances on the show. Yankovic has also supplied the voice of one-shot character 'Petroleum Joe' on _[The Brak Show](/wiki/The_Brak_Show "The Brak Show")_. He also voiced himself on a _[Back at the Barnyard](/wiki/Back_at_the_Barnyard "Back at the Barnyard")_ episode, and he appeared as a [ringmaster](/wiki/Ringmaster_\(circus\) "Ringmaster \(circus\)") who helps the regular characters of _[Yo Gabba Gabba!](/wiki/Yo_Gabba_Gabba! "Yo Gabba Gabba!")_ organize a [circus](/wiki/Circus "Circus") in a 2007 episode of the children's show. 

In 2011, Yankovic appeared as himself in the _[Batman: The Brave and the Bold](/wiki/Batman:_The_Brave_and_the_Bold "Batman: The Brave and the Bold")_ episode "[Bat-Mite](/wiki/Bat-Mite "Bat-Mite") Presents: Batman's Strangest Cases!"[191] In 2012, he appeared on two episodes of _[The Aquabats! Super Show!](/wiki/The_Aquabats!_Super_Show! "The Aquabats! Super Show!")_ , playing two different characters as the superhero SuperMagic PowerMan and as the president of the United States. In 2014, he appeared in the [fourth season](/wiki/My_Little_Pony:_Friendship_Is_Magic_\(season_4\) "My Little Pony: Friendship Is Magic \(season 4\)") _[My Little Pony: Friendship Is Magic](/wiki/My_Little_Pony:_Friendship_Is_Magic "My Little Pony: Friendship Is Magic")_ episode "[Pinkie Pride](/wiki/Pinkie_Pride "Pinkie Pride")" as [Cheese Sandwich](/wiki/Cheese_Sandwich_\(My_Little_Pony\) "Cheese Sandwich \(My Little Pony\)"), a rival party planner to Pinkie Pie.[192] He later reprised his role in the [season 9](/wiki/My_Little_Pony:_Friendship_Is_Magic_\(season_9\) "My Little Pony: Friendship Is Magic \(season 9\)") episode "The Last Laugh".[193]

In 2015, Yankovic voiced the supervillain [Darkseid](/wiki/Darkseid "Darkseid") in the _[Teen Titans Go!](/wiki/Teen_Titans_Go! "Teen Titans Go!")_ episode "Two Parter". He initially speaks with a deep, intimidating voice due to having a cold; after taking a [lozenge](/wiki/Throat_lozenge "Throat lozenge"), he speaks in his normal voice, and the heroes are no longer afraid of him. [Cyborg](/wiki/Cyborg_\(DC_Comics\) "Cyborg \(DC Comics\)") points out that Darkseid sounds like Yankovic, and the villain replies that he was "a true monster" for "undercutting musicians by subverting their words and compromising their artistic integrity". Cyborg objects to this, and they battle.[194]

In 2016, Yankovic appeared in two episodes of _[BoJack Horseman](/wiki/BoJack_Horseman "BoJack Horseman")_ as Mr. Peanutbutter's brother, Captain Peanutbutter, and began portraying Milo Murphy in the [Disney XD](/wiki/Disney_XD "Disney XD") series _[Milo Murphy's Law](/wiki/Milo_Murphy%27s_Law "Milo Murphy's Law")_.[195] Yankovic guest voiced as Papa Kotassium in a 2016 episode of Cartoon Network's animated series, _[Mighty Magiswords](/wiki/Mighty_Magiswords "Mighty Magiswords")_ , which was created by Weird Al fan, musician and accordionist Kyle Carrozza. Carrozza sent a FAQ to Weird Al when Carrozza was in college in 1999.[196]

### Web media

In 2008, Weird Al joined [Michael J. Nelson](/wiki/Michael_J._Nelson "Michael J. Nelson") as a guest on the [RiffTrax](/wiki/RiffTrax "RiffTrax") audio commentary of _[Jurassic Park](/wiki/Jurassic_Park_\(film\) "Jurassic Park \(film\)")_. On November 10, 2009, Weird Al was a guest "internet scientist" on [Rocketboom](/wiki/Rocketboom "Rocketboom")'s "[Know Your Meme](/wiki/Know_Your_Meme "Know Your Meme")" video series, in the installment on the topic of [Auto-Tune](/wiki/Auto-Tune "Auto-Tune"), hosted by Jamie Wilkinson. Yankovic later appeared in another _Funny or Die_ short alongside [Huey Lewis](/wiki/Huey_Lewis "Huey Lewis") which parodied the ax murder scene in the film _[American Psycho](/wiki/American_Psycho_\(film\) "American Psycho \(film\)")_ , in which [Christian Bale](/wiki/Christian_Bale "Christian Bale")'s character [Patrick Bateman](/wiki/Patrick_Bateman "Patrick Bateman") discusses the nature of Lewis's musical work before killing his victim.[197][198]

For _[The Nerdist Podcast](/wiki/The_Nerdist_Podcast "The Nerdist Podcast")_ , Weird Al began hosting a new comedic celebrity interview web series, _[Face to Face with 'Weird Al' Yankovic](/wiki/Face_to_Face_with_%22Weird_Al%22_Yankovic "Face to Face with "Weird Al" Yankovic")_ , on April 3, 2012. The series features _Al TV_ -esque fake interviews with film stars. Al has appeared on numerous other webshows, including _[CollegeHumor](/wiki/CollegeHumor "CollegeHumor")_ , _[LearningTown](/wiki/Paul_and_Storm "Paul and Storm")_ , _[Some Jerk with a Camera](/wiki/Some_Jerk_with_a_Camera "Some Jerk with a Camera")_ , _[Team Unicorn](/wiki/Team_Unicorn "Team Unicorn")_ , and _[Epic Rap Battles of History](/wiki/Epic_Rap_Battles_of_History "Epic Rap Battles of History")_ appearing as [Sir Isaac Newton](/wiki/Isaac_Newton "Isaac Newton") in a battle against actors portraying [Bill Nye](/wiki/Bill_Nye "Bill Nye"), the Science Guy (_YouTube_ star [Nice Peter](/wiki/Peter_Shukoff "Peter Shukoff")), and [Neil deGrasse Tyson](/wiki/Neil_deGrasse_Tyson "Neil deGrasse Tyson") ([Chali 2na](/wiki/Chali_2na "Chali 2na") of the group [Jurassic 5](/wiki/Jurassic_5 "Jurassic 5")).[199]

Yankovic has collobrated with the [Gregory Brothers](/wiki/Gregory_Brothers "Gregory Brothers") on music videos satirizing American [presidential election debates](/wiki/United_States_presidential_debates "United States presidential debates"). The first music video was released in October 2016, titled "Bad Hombres, Nasty Women", shortly after the [third debate](/wiki/United_States_presidential_election_debates,_2016#Third_presidential_debate_\(University_of_Nevada,_Las_Vegas\) "United States presidential election debates, 2016") between [Donald Trump](/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") and [Hillary Clinton](/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton"), with Yankovic singing between [autotuned](/wiki/Autotune "Autotune") snippets from the candidates.[200][201] Yankovic collaborated with the Gregory Brothers on a similar video, titled "We're All Doomed" after the [first debate](/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_debates#First_presidential_debate_\(Case_Western_Reserve_University\) "2020 United States presidential debates") in the 2020 campaign between Trump and [Joe Biden](/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden").[202] In 2024, Yankovic and the Gregory Brothers released "Deja Vu (But Worse)" about the [presidential debate](/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_debates "2024 United States presidential debates") in June 2024 between Trump and then-candidate Joe Biden.[203]

In 2018, Yankovic performed the theme song for the [Dropout](/wiki/Dropout_\(media_company\) "Dropout \(media company\)") animated web series _Cartoon Hell_.[204]

### Directing

Yankovic has directed many of his own music videos; he has directed all of his music videos from 1993's "Bedrock Anthem" to 2006's "White & Nerdy". He also directed the end sequence of 1986's "[Christmas at Ground Zero](/wiki/Christmas_at_Ground_Zero "Christmas at Ground Zero")" (an original piece juxtaposing Christmas with [nuclear warfare](/wiki/Nuclear_warfare "Nuclear warfare")) from his _[Polka Party!](/wiki/Polka_Party! "Polka Party!")_ album and the title sequence to _Spy Hard_ , for which he sang the title song.[205]

Yankovic wrote, directed and starred in the short 3-D film attraction "Al's Brain: A 3-D Journey Through The Human Brain", a $2.5 million project which was sponsored by and premiered at the [Orange County Fair](/wiki/Orange_County_Fair_\(California\) "Orange County Fair \(California\)") in [Costa Mesa, California](/wiki/Costa_Mesa,_California "Costa Mesa, California"), in 2009.[206] The project included a brief cameo by Sir [Paul McCartney](/wiki/Paul_McCartney "Paul McCartney"), which Yankovic directed during McCartney's appearance at the 2009 [Coachella Valley Music and Arts Festival](/wiki/Coachella_Valley_Music_and_Arts_Festival "Coachella Valley Music and Arts Festival").[207] Fair CEO Steve Beazley, who supported the project, considered the project a success and explored leasing the exhibit to other fairs; the second appearance of the exhibit was at the 2009 [Puyallup Fair](/wiki/Puyallup_Fair "Puyallup Fair") in Washington.[208]

He has also directed several videos for other artists, including [Hanson](/wiki/Hanson_\(band\) "Hanson \(band\)") (the _[Titanic](/wiki/Titanic_\(1997_film\) "Titanic \(1997 film\)")_ sequences in "River"), [The Black Crowes](/wiki/The_Black_Crowes "The Black Crowes") ("[Only a Fool](/wiki/Only_a_Fool_\(The_Black_Crowes_song\) "Only a Fool \(The Black Crowes song\)")"), Ben Folds ("[Rockin' the Suburbs](/wiki/Rockin%27_the_Suburbs_\(song\) "Rockin' the Suburbs \(song\)")"), [Jeff Foxworthy](/wiki/Jeff_Foxworthy "Jeff Foxworthy") ("Redneck Stomp" and "Party All Night"), [Jon Spencer Blues Explosion](/wiki/Jon_Spencer_Blues_Explosion "Jon Spencer Blues Explosion") ("Wail"), and [the Presidents of the United States of America](/wiki/The_Presidents_of_the_United_States_of_America_\(band\) "The Presidents of the United States of America \(band\)") ("Mixed Up S.O.B").[205] He has cameo appearances in his videos for Jon Spencer Blues Explosion, Hanson (as the interviewer), and Ben Folds (as the producer fixing Folds' "shitty tracks"). 

On January 25, 2010, Yankovic announced that he had signed a production deal with [Warner Bros.](/wiki/Warner_Bros. "Warner Bros.") to write and direct a live-action feature film for Cartoon Network.[209] Although Yankovic previously wrote the script for _UHF_ , this was to be the first film he directed.[209] Yankovic stated that he would not be starring in the film, as Cartoon Network wanted a younger protagonist. During an interview on _[Comedy Death-Ray Radio](/wiki/Comedy_Death-Ray_Radio "Comedy Death-Ray Radio")_ , Yankovic revealed that though Cartoon Network "loved" his script, the network decided that they were no longer intending to produce feature films. Yankovic initially stated that he would instead shop the script around to other potential studios,[210] but in 2013 revealed that the project had been scrapped as "it was really geared for Cartoon Network" and that he had "cannibalized jokes from that script to use for other projects".[211]

### Books

Yankovic wrote _When I Grow Up,_ a children's book released on February 1, 2011, and published by [HarperCollins](/wiki/HarperCollins "HarperCollins").[212] The book features 8-year-old Billy presenting to his class the wide variety of imaginative career possibilities that he is considering. Yankovic stated that the idea for the book was based on his own "circuitous" career path.[213] The book allows Yankovic to apply the humorous writing style found in his music in another medium, allowing him to use puns and rhymes.[213] Yankovic worked with HarperCollins' editor Anne Hoppe—the first time that Yankovic has had an editor—and found her help to be a positive experience.[213] The book is illustrated by Wes Hargis, who, according to Yankovic, has "a childlike quality and a very fun quality and a very imaginative quality" that matched well with Yankovic's writing.[213] The book reached the No. 4 position on [_The New York Times_ Best Seller list](/wiki/The_New_York_Times_Best_Seller_list "The New York Times Best Seller list") for Children's Picture Books for the week of February 20, 2011.[214]

Yankovic also wrote a sequel to _When I Grow Up_ , 2013's _My New Teacher and Me!_. 

Yankovic became the first guest editor for _[Mad](/wiki/Mad_\(magazine\) "Mad \(magazine\)")_ magazine for their 533rd issue, published in April 2015.[215]

Yankovic partnered with [Z2 Comics](/wiki/Z2_Comics "Z2 Comics") to produce _The Illustrated Al: The Songs of "Weird Al" Yankovic_ , which was released on January 19, 2023. The book takes twenty of Yankovic's songs and illustrates them in comic form, each by different artists. Yankovic selected artists for the book based on past experience in working with the artists, but short of proofing the final work, was otherwise hands-off for various editorial choices.[216] Following publication of _The Illustrated Al_ , Yankovic started working with the illustrators to produce new videos for these older songs, the first which was released in January 2023.[217]

### Other media

Yankovic competed on a week of _[Wheel of Fortune](/wiki/Wheel_of_Fortune_\(American_game_show\) "Wheel of Fortune \(American game show\)")_ taped at [Disney's Hollywood Studios](/wiki/Disney%27s_Hollywood_Studios "Disney's Hollywood Studios") in March 1994.[218] He also competed on _[Rock& Roll Jeopardy!](/wiki/Rock_%26_Roll_Jeopardy! "Rock & Roll Jeopardy!")_

Yankovic joined the band [Hanson](/wiki/Hanson_\(band\) "Hanson \(band\)") in their music video for "[Thinking 'bout Somethin'](/wiki/Thinking_%27bout_Somethin%27 "Thinking 'bout Somethin'")" in which he plays the tambourine. 

Yankovic contributes backing vocals for the song "Time" on Ben Folds' album _[Songs for Silverman](/wiki/Songs_for_Silverman "Songs for Silverman")_. 

Yankovic was also one of many celebrities who took part in the [NOH8 Campaign](/wiki/NOH8_Campaign "NOH8 Campaign") against [Proposition 8](/wiki/California_Proposition_8 "California Proposition 8"), which banned [same-sex marriage](/wiki/Same-sex_marriage "Same-sex marriage") in California.[219]

Yankovic was approached by a beer company to endorse their product. Yankovic had turned it down because he believed that "a lot of my fans were young and impressionable".[220] Yankovic later posted on his Twitter account that he never regretted the decision.[221]

In 2009, Yankovic was a special guest on an episode of [G4](/wiki/G4_\(American_TV_network\) "G4 \(American TV network\)")'s _[Web Soup](/wiki/Web_Soup "Web Soup")_ where he came as [Mark Gormley](/wiki/Mark_Gormley "Mark Gormley") at first.[222]

In 2011, Yankovic guest starred as the character "Banana Man" in an episode of _[Adventure Time](/wiki/Adventure_Time "Adventure Time")_. The same year, he appeared as himself in the _[How I Met Your Mother](/wiki/How_I_Met_Your_Mother "How I Met Your Mother")_ episode "[Noretta](/wiki/Noretta "Noretta")". 

In 2012, he appeared as himself along with [Alice Cooper](/wiki/Alice_Cooper "Alice Cooper"), [Bret Michaels](/wiki/Bret_Michaels "Bret Michaels"), and [Maria Menounos](/wiki/Maria_Menounos "Maria Menounos") in _[The High Fructose Adventures of Annoying Orange](/wiki/The_High_Fructose_Adventures_of_Annoying_Orange "The High Fructose Adventures of Annoying Orange")_ for the Christmas special, and sung with Alice, Bret, and Orange. 

On May 31, 2014, Yankovic won the ACE Award (Amateur Cartoonist Extraordinaire) from the National Cartoonists Society at its awards banquet in San Diego.[223]

From 2014 until 2017, Yankovic appeared as a celebrity contestant in eight episodes of the game show _[Celebrity Name Game](/wiki/Celebrity_Name_Game_\(American_game_show\) "Celebrity Name Game \(American game show\)")_.[224]

On November 19, 2014, a [RadioShack](/wiki/RadioShack "RadioShack") ad was uploaded to YouTube which featured Al in the role of a RadioShack employee.[225]

In 2015, Yankovic made an appearance on an episode of _[The Odd Couple](/wiki/The_Odd_Couple_\(2015_TV_series\) "The Odd Couple \(2015 TV series\)")_ as a yoga student in the class Felix takes over for one day. 

Also in 2015, Yankovic was featured as _Mad_ magazine's first ever guest editor for their 533rd issue.[226]

In 2016, Yankovic became the bandleader on the [IFC](/wiki/IFC_\(American_TV_channel\) "IFC \(American TV channel\)") series _[Comedy Bang! Bang!](/wiki/Comedy_Bang!_Bang!_\(TV_series\) "Comedy Bang! Bang! \(TV series\)")_ , on which he had previously guest starred.[227]

In the official video for [Weezer](/wiki/Weezer "Weezer")'s cover of "[Africa](/wiki/Africa_\(song\)#Weezer_version "Africa \(song\)")" published in September 2018, which itself is parody of Weezer's video for "[Undone – The Sweater Song](/wiki/Undone_%E2%80%93_The_Sweater_Song "Undone – The Sweater Song")", Yankovic stands in for [Rivers Cuomo](/wiki/Rivers_Cuomo "Rivers Cuomo") as vocalist and lead guitar.[228]

Though he does not appear, Yankovic is mentioned directly by name in the 2021 television series _[Y: The Last Man](/wiki/Y:_The_Last_Man_\(TV_series\) "Y: The Last Man \(TV series\)")_ adapted from the 2000s comic book series of the same name, which involves a post-apocalyptic alternative timeline where all the men of the world had died. Whereas the comic book had the characters reflect on the absence of the Rolling Stones, showrunner [Eliza Clark](/wiki/Eliza_Clark_\(American_writer\) "Eliza Clark \(American writer\)") opted to update the references for the show, and used Yankovic as a more modern artist that had been considered a great loss.[229]

In 2025, Yankovic appeared in the music video for "Terrapin" by American indie musician [Clairo](/wiki/Clairo "Clairo"), in which he plays Clairo. The music video was directed by American actress [Ayo Edebiri](/wiki/Ayo_Edebiri "Ayo Edebiri"), marking Yankovic's first collaboration with either of them.[230]

Yankovic has cameos in all four of _[The Naked Gun](/wiki/The_Naked_Gun "The Naked Gun")_ films. 

## Misattribution and imitators

[](/wiki/File:WeirdAlLimeWire.png)A screenshot of [LimeWire](/wiki/LimeWire "LimeWire") PRO, showing a large number of parodies misattributed to Yankovic, as well as numerous misspellings of his surname (February 2007 (2007-02))

Songs posted to [file-sharing](/wiki/File_sharing "File sharing") networks are often misattributed to him because of their humorous subject matter. Often, his surname is misspelled (and thus mispronounced) as "Yankovich", among other variations. Much to the disdain of Yankovic, these misattributed files include songs that are racist, sexually explicit, or otherwise offensive. A young listener who had heard several of these offensive tracks by way of a file-sharing service confronted Yankovic online, threatening a boycott because of his supposedly explicit lyrics.[231] Quite a few of the songs, such as "[Star Wars Cantina](/wiki/Star_Wars_Cantina_\(song\)#Parody_song "Star Wars Cantina \(song\)")" by [Mark Jonathan Davis](/wiki/Mark_Jonathan_Davis "Mark Jonathan Davis") (not, in a double misattribution, his lounge-singer character [Richard Cheese](/wiki/Richard_Cheese "Richard Cheese")), "[Star Wars Gangsta Rap](/wiki/Star_Wars_Gangsta_Rap "Star Wars Gangsta Rap")", "Yoda Smokes Weed", "Chewbacca", "[The Devil Went to Jamaica](/wiki/The_Devil_Went_Down_to_Georgia#Parody_song "The Devil Went Down to Georgia")", and several more have a _[Star Wars](/wiki/Star_Wars "Star Wars")_ motif.[232] Some songs misattributed to him are not songs, but spoken skits, such as "[Sesame Street](/wiki/Sesame_Street "Sesame Street") on Crack", which is also widely misattributed to [Adam Sandler](/wiki/Adam_Sandler "Adam Sandler"). A list of songs frequently misattributed to Yankovic can be found at The Not Al Page[232] and a list of all commercially released songs recorded by Yankovic can be found on his website.[233]

Yankovic cites these misattributions as his only real problem with [peer-to-peer file-sharing](/wiki/Peer-to-peer_file_sharing "Peer-to-peer file sharing") sites: 

> If you do a search for my name on any one of those sites, I guarantee you that about half of the songs that come up will be songs I had absolutely nothing to do with. That particularly bothers me, because I really try to do quality work, and I also try to maintain a more-or-less family-friendly image—and some of these songs that are supposedly by me are just, well, vulgar and awful. I truly think my reputation has suffered in a lot of people's minds because of all those fake Weird Al songs floating around the Internet.[234]

In terms of legitimate parodies of Yankovic, the _[Mr. Show](/wiki/Mr._Show_with_Bob_and_David "Mr. Show with Bob and David")_ sketch "Superstar Machine" features [Bob Odenkirk](/wiki/Bob_Odenkirk "Bob Odenkirk") as the character Daffy "Mal" Yinkleyankle. Yankovic was impressed by the parody, and stated that it "zeroed in on everything that's irritating about me".[235][236]

## Fan-driven campaigns

The Weird Al Star Fund was a campaign started by Yankovic's fans to get him a star on the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame "Hollywood Walk of Fame"). Their mission was to "solicit, collect, and raise the necessary money, and to compile the information needed for the application to nominate "Weird Al" Yankovic for a star on the Hollywood Walk of Fame".[237] Fans worldwide have sent donations to raise the US$40,000 needed for a nomination. In addition to the preferred method of cash donations, many methods were used to raise money for the cause, such as a live benefit show held April 11, 2006, and selling merchandise on the official website and [eBay](/wiki/EBay "EBay"), including T-shirts, calendars, and cookbooks.[238] On May 26, 2006, the campaign hit the then-$15,000 target, just five days before the May 31 deadline to submit the necessary paperwork.[237] However, Yankovic was not included on the list of inductees for 2007.[239] On February 9, 2007, the Hollywood Chamber Of Commerce raised the price to sponsor a new star to $25,000.[237] Yankovic's application was resubmitted for consideration in 2007, but he was not included among 2008's inductees.[240] In December 2010, the price was raised again to $30,000.[237] and in 2017 the price was raised to $40,000.[237] The campaign raised the new target each time and applications continued to be submitted yearly, until The Hollywood Chamber of Commerce announced in June 2017 that Yankovic would receive a star on the Walk of Fame as one of the 2018 inductees. In an official induction ceremony on August 27, 2018,[241] Al received the 2,643rd star on the Hollywood Walk of Fame. The star is located at 6914 Hollywood Blvd, directly across the street from [TCL Chinese Theatre](/wiki/TCL_Chinese_Theatre "TCL Chinese Theatre"). The ceremony was attended by 1,500 fans.[242]

Similar to the Weird Al Star Fund, a second fan-driven campaign called "Make the Rock Hall 'Weird'" has tried to enshrine him into the [Rock and Roll Hall of Fame](/wiki/Rock_and_Roll_Hall_of_Fame "Rock and Roll Hall of Fame") in [Cleveland](/wiki/Cleveland "Cleveland"), Ohio, for which he has been eligible since 2004.[243] Previous attempts to raise awareness for the campaign and support Yankovic's nomination included a petition drive from 2006 to 2007, which raised over 9000 signatures; an art competition in 2005; additionally, a documentary film about the campaign is currently being developed.[244][245] In addition to these efforts, an ongoing campaign is underway in which supporters of Yankovic's nomination are requested to send "sincere, thoughtful" letters to the Rock Hall Foundation's headquarters in New York.[245] The Hall has not considered Yankovic for nomination since the campaign started in 2004.[243] A 2009 _Rolling Stone_ poll named Weird Al as the top artist that should be nominated for the Hall of Fame, followed by [Rush](/wiki/Rush_\(band\) "Rush \(band\)") (who were inducted in 2013) and [The Moody Blues](/wiki/The_Moody_Blues "The Moody Blues") (inducted in 2018) in the top ten.[246] According to the museum's chairperson [John Sykes](/wiki/John_Sykes_\(entertainment_industry_executive\) "John Sykes \(entertainment industry executive\)"), while Yankovic has come up in various discussions on nominees, "he's never made it close to the ballot" due to the artists that vote towards the inductees.[247]

A smaller ongoing effort has been made by fans to have Yankovic perform at the halftime show of a [Super Bowl](/wiki/Super_Bowl "Super Bowl") game.[248] This inspired Yankovic to write the [fight song](/wiki/Fight_song "Fight song") parody "Sports Song" for _Mandatory Fun_ to help round out his repertoire.[249] Subsequent to the success of _Mandatory Fun_ , another fan-driven campaign pushed for Yankovic to headline the then-upcoming [Super Bowl XLIX](/wiki/Super_Bowl_XLIX "Super Bowl XLIX") at the highlight of the artist's career, which was noticed by many media outlets, including [CNN](/wiki/CNN "CNN") and [Wired](/wiki/Wired_\(magazine\) "Wired \(magazine\)"), though the decision for this selection would reside within the management of the [NFL](/wiki/NFL "NFL") (who instead chose [Katy Perry](/wiki/Katy_Perry "Katy Perry") for that position).[250][251][252] Yankovic believed that he would never be selected as a standalone act for the Super Bowl, but could see other bands include him as a special guest if they were selected.[253]

## Band members

Yankovic's core band, consisting of Jon "Bermuda" Schwartz, Jim West, and Steve Jay, have performed with him for more than 40 years, and grew with the addition of Rubén Valtierra. Yankovic credits this group as having the flexibility to play any musical genre, allowing him to perform songs across a wide range of musical styles, in addition to "we're kind of telepathic in the way we communicate now, so we're a lot better than we were back in the day". With his 2025 "Bigger & Weirder tour, Yankovic added four additional members, Probyn Gregory, Scheila Gonzalez, Payton Velligan, and Monique Donnelly, all together that add a range of additional instruments that Yankovic can incorporate, in addition for three-part female vocal harmony with Gonzalez, Velligan, and Donnelly.[106]

**Current members**

  * "Weird Al" Yankovic – lead vocals, [accordion](/wiki/Accordion "Accordion"), keyboards, occasional live percussion (1976–present)
  * [Jon "Bermuda" Schwartz](/wiki/Jon_Schwartz_\(drummer\) "Jon Schwartz \(drummer\)") – drums, percussion, backing vocals (1980–present)
  * [Jim "Kimo" West](/wiki/Jim_West_\(guitarist\) "Jim West \(guitarist\)") – guitar, [mandolin](/wiki/Mandolin "Mandolin"), backing vocals, occasional banjo (1983–present)
  * [Steve Jay](/wiki/Steve_Jay "Steve Jay") – bass, [banjo](/wiki/Banjo "Banjo"), backing vocals, occasional live keyboards (1983–present)
  * [Rubén Valtierra](/wiki/Rub%C3%A9n_Valtierra "Rubén Valtierra") – piano, keyboards, backing vocals, occasional live percussion (1991–present)



**Touring members**

  * Probyn Gregory – backing vocals, trumpet, trombone, tuba, guitars, percussion (2025–present)[265][266][267]
  * [Scheila Gonzalez](/wiki/Scheila_Gonzalez "Scheila Gonzalez") – backing vocals, saxophone, clarinet, percussion (2025–present)[265][266][267]
  * Payton Velligan – backing vocals, guitars, percussion (2025–present)[265][266][267]
  * Monique Donnelly – backing vocals, keyboards, percussion (2025–present)[265][266][267]



**Former members**

  * [Rick Derringer](/wiki/Rick_Derringer "Rick Derringer") – guitar, occasional mandolin, backing vocals (1982–1990; died 2025)



**Timeline**

  *[POTUS  
No.]: Number
  *[b.]: born
  *[q.v.]: see also
  *[25]: Not actually given in Africanus; the number is deduced from Manetho's other copyists, as well as Africanus' own sum of years.
  *[69 years]: The correct sum should be 97; the total excluding Queen Hatshepsut is 75 years.
  *[263 years]: This is actually the sum of the first 13 kings, which amounts to 262 years. Adding the reign of the 14th king raises the number to 267 years.
  *[348 years]: The sum of the first thirteen kings is 263 years, just as Africanus. The "correct" sum of years would be 376.
  *[41 years]: Africanus gives 46 years, but this does not match the stated total.
  *[35 years]: Africanus gives 14 years, but this does not match the stated total.
  *[ 120 years]: Africanus' sum of items comes to 130 years.
  *[40 years]: Eusebius gives 25 years
  *[8 years]: Eusebius gives 12 years
  *[ 40 years]: Eusebius gives 44 years
  *[14 years]: Eusebius gives 12 years
  *[18 years]: Eusebius gives 20 years
